Since this impropriety occurred, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar performs the appropriate expiatory actions. Having done so, he completes the puja and returns to his home. Sekkizhar Peruman sings, "The devotee of the Lord with the matted hair entered his protected home." Sekkizhar Peruman refers to his home as "kadi manai" (protected home). "Kadi" means protection. It is stated here that the Nayanar entered his protected home.

Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's house is a house protected by the never-extinguishing sacred fire and the Vedic discipline that is always nurtured according to the rules. Furthermore, that night, the Lord appeared in Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's dream and spoke of the greatness of Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's wife's love, making Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's house special. Moreover, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's house was blessed to have Thirugnanasambandha Peruman and his retinue visit later. Therefore, Sekkizhar Peruman refers to Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's house as "kadi manai" to encompass all these. "Kadi" also means "scolded," implying that he, who had scolded his wife, entered that house. This is also implied here. Thus, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar went to his house and stayed there. Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 08 — Tiruneelanakkar Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam by Sekkizhar Swamigal, Part 523, Thirunindra Sarukkam, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar Puranam. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar returns to his house. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's wife, filled with great fear, does not approach Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar but remains in the temple. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's wife, thinking that she had caused an impropriety to the Lord and that her husband had consequently renounced her, remains in the temple, filled with fear. The verse says: "With a fearful heart, she could not approach him. The lady stayed in the temple of Him who drank poison. Thiruneelanakkar, the master of pure four Vedas, slept on a soft cotton bed that night." Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's wife, having been renounced by her bodily husband, remains in the temple of Lord Shiva, her spiritual husband. Sekkizhar Peruman sings this as "The lady stayed in the temple of Him who drank poison." It is a worldly dharma that women should not stay anywhere other than their husband's house. This is explained here: when Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's wife was renounced by her bodily husband, she stayed with Lord Shiva, her spiritual husband. She stayed in His temple. Lord Shiva is one who drank poison. Just as He transformed that poison into nectar, so too did Lord Shiva consider the impropriety committed by Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar, due to love, as an excellent act. In the temple of such a Lord Shiva, the lady, the best among women, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's wife, stays. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar, without giving any thought to having renounced his wife, sleeps in his house. It says "palli kolgindraar" (rests) instead of "thoonginaar" (slept). "Palli kolgindraar" means that even though he was no longer awake, he did not completely surrender to sleep but rested with the thought of Lord Shiva. Now, the next verses, two continuous verses: "As he was resting, Lord Ayavanthi, in his dream, showed his form with matted hair carrying white water and said, 'Look at these blisters caused by the spider on this side, except for the part where you blew with love.' Having seen that great dream, he considered it a reality. With fear, he clasped his hands in reverence, awoke, and the devotee worshipped, danced, sang, and praised. He wept, praising the compassion of the Lord of the celestials." Thus, as Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar was resting, he was not oblivious but was thinking of Lord Shiva. However, having departed from the waking state, he was then in a dream state. In the Nayanar's dream, Lord Shiva appeared, showing His form with matted hair carrying the Ganges, the white water. Thus, Lord Shiva appeared in Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's dream and showed His sacred form. Showing His sacred form, He said, "Look, the part where your wife blew with great love on My sacred form is perfectly fine. But look at the blisters caused by the spider on this side, except for the part where your wife blew." Thus, Lord Shiva showed this in Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's dream. On Lord Shiva's sacred form, in the area where Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's wife, out of great love, had blown, allowing her saliva to touch, there were no blisters. On the side of Lord Shiva's sacred form where Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's wife had not blown, there were blisters. Lord Shiva showed this to Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar with His matted hair carrying the Ganges. Earlier, the sacred form that Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar saw as having an impropriety was the same sacred form. Now, the sacred form shown is also the same. But earlier, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar saw it without considering his wife's love, that is, without considering the love his wife had for the Lord. But now, the Lord showed it to Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar so that he could see the greatness of his wife's love. "Who can see if the Lord does not show? Who can see, O Lord with the eye on the forehead, if you do not show?" is a sacred saying from Thevaram. Sekkizhar Peruman here refers to Lord Shiva as "Lord Shiva with the matted hair carrying water" when describing the showing of the sacred form. The Lord carries the Ganges water. Therefore, the Lord has a sacred form that is always bathed by the Ganges water. The Ganges is considered the best among holy waters, capable of removing all ills, including the disease of birth, and protecting. Such Ganges water always bathes the Lord's sacred form. Even with such Ganges water, the blisters caused by the spider on the Lord's sacred form could not be removed. But the water that came from Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's mouth, that saliva-mixed water, when it touched the Lord's sacred form, the blisters in that area were removed. Therefore, the water that came from Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's wife's mouth, mixed with saliva, due to love, became holier than the Ganges water. The Lord accepted it as holier than the Ganges water. To indicate this, Sekkizhar Peruman sings here that the Lord showed His form with matted hair carrying water in the dream. The divine words of divine Sekkizhar are the very words of the Lord, and this is an excellent example of that. Just as in the Puranam of Kannappa Nayanar, Kannappa Nayanar filled his mouth with the water of the Ponnimugali river and spat it on the Lord's sacred form, performing ablution. Therefore, that water was praised in the 671st verse of Kannappa Nayanar's Puranam as "the sacred water from the mouth, holier than the holy Ganges water." Similarly, here, when Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's wife blew to remove the spider that fell on the Shiva Linga form, the water that spurted from her mouth, the Lord indicates here that He accepted it as holier than the Ganges water. Thus, when the Lord spoke in Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's dream, he considered that great dream a reality, thinking it was a direct vision. With fear, he clasped his hands in reverence and awoke. This was a great dream. A dream that conveyed greatness. A dream granted by Lord Shiva, the great one. Therefore, without thinking that what he saw in the dream state was merely beneficial, but considering it a direct vision in the waking state, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar awoke with his hands clasped in reverence. Then he worshipped the Lord, danced, and sang. All these happened continuously due to the manifestation of divine grace. These events happened due to the abundance of love. He sang the glories of the Lord as hymns.

He praised the Lord, extolling His greatness. He wept, praising the compassion of the Lord. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar wept, contemplating the greatness of the divine grace that considered his wife's impropriety as proper. He also wept, contemplating the greatness of the Lord's grace, who, despite his own error in judgment, considered him worthy. "The Lord who forgave the impropriety committed by the great devotees out of boundless love, and who is always the Lord," is a sacred saying from Thevaram. This applies here to Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar and his wife. It reveals the immense grace of the Lord. When contemplating the Lord's greatness and one's own insignificance, and when contemplating His greatness in bestowing grace upon one despite one's insignificance, devotees lament, cry out, weep, and worship. These are the emotional expressions that occur in Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar here. As this was happening, the night passed and dawn broke. After seeing that great dream where the Lord appeared, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar did not sleep but waited for dawn. Therefore, as soon as dawn broke, the Nayanar's first action was to enter the temple. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar now understood the greatness of his wife. Therefore, as soon as dawn broke, he went to the temple, worshipped Lord Shiva, and then brought his wife, who was there, back to his house. Here, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's wife is referred to as "Madharar" (lady) with the honorific suffix "ar" added to "Madhar" (woman) in this verse. This indicates that the Nayanar had realized the greatness of his wife. Upon returning, he felt greater joy than before, and with increasing delight, he performed countless pujas to the Lord. Similarly, as many loving devotees arrived, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar continued to provide them with whatever they needed, as before, with great willingness. As the Lord's divine grace manifested in this way, the greatness of Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's love became known to the world. Therefore, more devotees than before came to Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar continued to provide them with whatever they needed, as before, with great willingness and love. As he was living in this manner, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar heard the world praising and extolling the greatness of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, who appeared in Sirkazhi. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar desired to worship the sacred feet of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman and to be with him. Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 09 — Tiruneelanakkar Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam by Sekkizhar Swamigal, Part 524, Thirunindra Sarukkam, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar Puranam. The next verse is: "As Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar was living in such a state of grace and devotion, the greatness of the Brahmin child who came from the enduring Poondharai spread throughout the world. He placed his head on his feet and excelled in the desire to be with him." Thus, as Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar was performing service to the Lord and service to the devotees, the greatness of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, who was born in Sirkazhi, spread throughout the world. The whole world praised Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. Sekkizhar Peruman composes this in the verse as "the greatness of the Brahmin child who came from the enduring Poondharai spread throughout the world, and the world praised him." Poondharai is one of the 12 sacred names of Sirkazhi. It is called "Manna Poondharai" (enduring Poondharai). "Mannuthal" means to endure. Even during the great floods in many eons, Sirkazhi remained afloat and stable without being submerged. Therefore, it is called "Manna Poondharai." Thirugnanasambandha Peruman was born there. It is a Saiva tradition to refer to Thirugnanasambandha Peruman as "Aludaiya Pillaiyar" (the child who possesses). The reason for Thirugnanasambandha Peruman receiving the name "Pillaiyar" is explained in the 69th verse of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman's Puranam. We will contemplate it there. Thus, the greatness of the child who was born is praised by the whole world. It is true that "the world refers to the exalted ones." Therefore, if the world praises him, it means that the intelligent people in the world praise the greatness of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. They praise him in various ways. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar hears this. Therefore, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar desires to worship the sacred feet of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar desires to see Thirugnanasambandha Peruman directly and prostrate before him. Moreover, he desires to be with Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. Now, the next verse: "As the great ascetic, who was of noble character, was living in such a state, the king of Champa, Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, who worshipped the Lord with the matted hair carrying the sky-high water in many good holy places to receive the fruit of great penance, arrived in Saaththamangai." Thus, as Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar was living for a long time in such a state of devotion to the Lord and His devotees, Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, the king of Sirkazhi, was undertaking pilgrimages. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's desire to see Thirugnanasambandha Peruman directly and worship his sacred feet had matured and was now ready to bear fruit. Therefore, Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, who was undertaking pilgrimages to various holy places, arrived at Thiruchaththamangai, where Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar lived. Thirugnanasambandha Peruman was undertaking pilgrimages to receive the fruit of great penance. Out of compassion for the people of this world, Lord Shiva manifests Himself in holy places so that the people of this world may see Him, worship Him, and attain salvation. Great ascetics of earlier times would visit these places and benefit. "The celestials also come to earth and worship the Lord," is a sacred saying. "We are wasting our days by not being born on earth," is a sacred saying from Thiruvachagam. According to these sayings, this earth is a suitable place for performing penance. This earth is a place conducive to being born and performing penance. Therefore, the fruit of great penance for living beings is to visit the places where the Lord is enshrined and worship Him. This is also considered the greatest among all penances. Therefore, Thirugnanasambandha Peruman visited the places where the Lord was enshrined and worshipped Him, composing patikams. Thirugnanasambandha Peruman's worship in all Shiva temples was not for himself but for the benefit of the people of this world. Thus, in the course of visiting and worshipping many holy places, Thirugnanasambandha Peruman arrived at Thiruchaththamangai. The next two verses are: "Hearing that the king of Pugali, accompanied by the great Thiruneelakanda Perum Panar, whose fame was widespread, and his wife, the விரலியார் (Viraliyar) with ear-adorned tresses, and a large gathering of devotees, had arrived near Saaththamangai, Neelanakkar rejoiced in his heart. Immediately, with great joy, he set up garlands and flags, erected ornamental arches, and made a long covered pathway. He, along with his large retinue, went forth to welcome them." Sekkizhar Peruman describes how Thirugnanasambandha Peruman arrived. Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, the leader of Sirkazhi, arrived at the border of Thiruchaththamangai, accompanied by the great Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar, whose fame was widespread, and his wife, Mathanga Chulamani Ammaiyar, and a large gathering of devotees. Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar and his wife Mathanga Chulamani Ammaiyar always accompanied Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar was blessed with the privilege of serving Aludaiya Pillaiyar. He still possesses that privilege. He is about to receive even greater honor. Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar and his wife Mathanga Chulamani Ammaiyar always accompanied Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. Therefore, wherever Thirugnanasambandha Peruman went, both of them would accompany him. In this verse, Sekkizhar Peruman refers to Mathanga Chulamani Ammaiyar as "Viraliyar." "Viraliyar" is a traditional name for women born in the Panar community. It means a female singer. Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar and Mathanga Chulamani Ammaiyar were born into the Panar community. They were skilled in music. They would sing and play the Lord's patikams with their voices and instruments, thus coming forward in Thirugnanasambandha Peruman's retinue as a couple. Both of them would always accompany Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. They would sing the patikams composed by Thirugnanasambandha Peruman with melodies. Moreover, they were great devotees of Shiva and had great devotion to Thirugnanasambandha Peruman as their Guru and to the devotees. Therefore, they were accustomed to coming close to Thirugnanasambandha Peruman in his retinue. That is why Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar is one of the 63 Nayanmars and blesses us. We will extensively see the glories of these two in the Puranams of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman and Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar. Their history is also included in the Puranam of Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar. Therefore, thus, the great gathering of Pillaiyar and countless attendants and devotees who joined them from various places gathered at the border of Thiruchaththamangai. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar hears this. Upon hearing it, he rejoices greatly. He arranges garlands and flags as decorations.

He erects ornamental arches. He constructs a long covered pathway. That is, he arranges a good canopy and clears the road wherever Thirugnanasambandha Peruman and his great retinue are to walk, making it cool and comfortable. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's long-awaited and meditated upon desire is now coming to fruition. Therefore, as soon as he hears that Gnanasambandha Peruman is coming with his retinue, he immediately, without a moment's delay, engages in excellent activities to welcome Gnanasambandha Peruman. This shows us the intensity of his love for the Lord. Therefore, as mentioned above, he constructs a long canopy. He constructs a walking canopy. He constructs a long walking canopy so that those who come do not feel tired and can walk in the shade and coolness. Similarly, Sivaneyar, who lived in Thirumayilappur, hearing that Thirugnanasambandha Peruman was coming to Thirumayilai, similarly constructed a walking canopy from Thiruvottriyur to Thirumayilappur to welcome him. We are reminded here of Sivaneyar's act of love. Having made all these arrangements, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar waits there with his large retinue to welcome Thirugnanasambandha Peruman and his retinue. He goes to meet them. "He joined the retinue of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman and welcomed him there. Overwhelmed with joy, he danced, sang, worshipped, rose, and brought them all into his golden, shining, great house." He joins the retinue of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman and welcomes him there. Due to that joy, he dances, sings, worships, and bows down. Thus, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's emotional expressions occur due to his darshan of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman and his joining the retinue. Thus, he welcomes them all and takes them to his house. The next verse is: "His house became worthy of the greatness of Pillaiyar's arrival. With his heart overflowing with love for the flood-like multitude of devotees, he served sacred food to the generous Lord of Sirkazhi in his house." Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's house becomes a house worthy of the greatness of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman's arrival. A large multitude of devotees always accompanies Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. That multitude of devotees is like a river flood, gathering and increasing from many directions, coming incessantly. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar rejoices in having such a large multitude of devotees in his house. Generally, those who feed and worship devotees might feel a little disheartened if more devotees arrive than expected. But our Nayanmars, the more the crowd, the more they rejoice. "Even if they come in multitudes, if they see devotees, they will serve them with good qualities," says Kulachirai Nayanar. "The loving one, Ilaiyankudi Mara Nayanar, fed sacred food to the devotees who came in increasing numbers daily, so that their hearts might rejoice." Thus, when a very large multitude of devotees arrived, our Nayanmars had the quality of welcoming them with overflowing joy and serving them sacred food. Thus, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar served sacred food to Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, the generous Lord of Sirkazhi, who came with that retinue, in his house. Sekkizhar Peruman refers to Thirugnanasambandha Peruman as "Vallalar" (generous one) in this verse. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar desired to have darshan of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman and to be with him. But Thirugnanasambandha Peruman himself came to the holy place where Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar lived and accepted his service. Now, Thirugnanasambandha Peruman is going to accept the service of Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar and the entire retinue in his sacred marriage and bestow liberation. Therefore, Sekkizhar Peruman refers to Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, the king of Sirkazhi, as "Vallalar" in this verse. Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 10 — Tiruneelanakkar Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam by Sekkizhar Swamigal, Part 525, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar Puranam. Thirugnanasambandha Peruman and the accompanying retinue of devotees partake of sacred food at Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's house. They stay there all day. Then evening comes. The sun sets in the western sea. The sun has a name "Pagalan" (day-maker). He is called Pagalan because he divides the day into day and night. "Pagu" refers to dividing. Therefore, the sun is called Pagalan because he divides the day into day and night. He sets in the western sea. Due to the rotation of the earth, the western sea moves towards the sun. It appears to us as if the sun sets in the western sea. Therefore, saying that the sun sets in the western sea is a figure of speech. Thus, as the day passes, night comes. The night comes when the cool rays of the moon reach the ponds where water lilies and kumudha flowers bloom. Therefore, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar requests Thirugnanasambandha Peruman to stay there for the night. As he agrees, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar arranges the place for Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, who drank the milk from the breast of the daughter of the Himalayas, to rest for the night. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar also serves sacred food and other things. Thirugnanasambandha Peruman partakes of sacred food with the virtuous true devotees that night. Thirugnanasambandha Peruman is one who wept for the world to be saved, for the Lord to appear with the Great Goddess. That is, he wept for the world to be saved, for Lord Shiva to appear with the Great Goddess, and for that time to come. He also wept for the world to be saved. Because he wept, he drank the milk of wisdom from the Goddess. Therefore, the nectar-like Thevaram hymns were composed by him. That is the reason for the world's salvation. While Thirugnanasambandha Peruman was there, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar was making arrangements for Thirugnanasambandha Peruman and the retinue of devotees to stay for the night. At that time, Thirugnanasambandha Peruman calls the Nayanar. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar quickly comes to the place where Thirugnanasambandha Peruman is. He stands there humbly, prostrating, with a look that asks what the command is. When elders call, one should stand humbly and prostrate before them, rather than asking with words why they called. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar demonstrates this good tradition here. Thirugnanasambandha Peruman says to Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar, who is standing humbly and prostrating: "Will you graciously provide a place for Thiruneelakanda Perum Panar, who stands with love, to stay tonight?" This is the verse. This is a very important verse in the histories of Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar, Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar, and Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar is standing there, worshipping Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, looking at Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar, says, "Please graciously provide a good place for Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar to stay here tonight." Thirugnanasambandha Peruman asks this. Why does Thirugnanasambandha Peruman say this here? Because Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar always accompanies Thirugnanasambandha Peruman with his wife, Mathanga Chulamani Ammaiyar. Therefore, Thirugnanasambandha Peruman asks Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar to arrange a good place for Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar, who comes with his wife, to stay. Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar is one who comes with his wife. Furthermore, Thirugnanasambandha Peruman explains to Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar the greatness and devotion of Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar and the love Thirugnanasambandha Peruman has for him. Therefore, Thirugnanasambandha Peruman asks Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar in this way, with the intention that a place should be provided for both of them to stay as he wishes. Hearing this, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar rejoices greatly, as he has the same intention. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar rejoices greatly that his thought and Thirugnanasambandha Peruman's sacred intention are perfectly aligned. Therefore, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar, the sacred Brahmin, arranges for Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar and Mathanga Chulamani Ammaiyar to stay near the altar in the middle of his house where the sacrificial fire is nurtured. Here, some people will conduct unnecessary research. Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar's lineage is Panar lineage. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's lineage is Vedic lineage. Therefore, in those days, people belonging to the Panar community, who sang songs, would be asked to stay in their designated places. This is because he is a Vedic scholar, and many people have conducted various unnecessary researches about caste here. But all of them are not true. This is because caste, lineage, language, and race are all things that exist and perish at the worldly level. For our Nayanmars, who are great devotees of Shiva, who are Samochitta (equal-minded) and in a high Shiva state, these ordinary worldly customs do not apply. They are those who are in a state of intense Sakti Nipada Pakkuvam (maturity of divine grace), transcending all these. Therefore, conducting such unnecessary research about such souls, who are in a state of intense maturity, according to our own level, is a futile and improper task. Now, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar places Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar and Mathanga Chulamani Ammaiyar near the place where the sacrificial fire is nurtured in the middle of his house. At that time, the sacrificial fire grew and swirled clockwise. Sekkizhar Peruman describes this in the next verse. The next verse is: "The never-extinguishing sacred fire in the altar swirled clockwise, glowing more brightly than before. The scholars rejoiced, and the leader of the Sagoda Yazh, with his wife, rested there by grace." The sacrificial fire in Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's house is a never-extinguishing sacrificial fire. It is a fire that is always burning. Thus, when Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar and his wife stayed near the altar, that fire, which always grew incessantly, shone brightly in many ways, more than its usual state of constant glow. It glowed brightly, radiating light. It burned high. It spread widely. It swirled. And it swirled clockwise. It burned much more intensely than usual. This increased brilliance, more than before, is as if to clearly demonstrate the devotion of Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar and the chastity of Mathanga Chulamani Ammaiyar, and the fire grows because of them.

The sacrificial fire swirling and growing clockwise generally indicates auspiciousness. It is said that burning counter-clockwise indicates bad omens, such as foul odors. That sacrificial fire in Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's house is an ever-burning sacrificial fire. It is called Nithyagni (eternal fire). This fire is of three types: Ahavaniyam, Garhapatyam, and Dakshinagni. It is a tradition that Brahmins must protect this fire from extinguishing until their death. We have already contemplated all this. Therefore, such a sacrificial fire shines brightly there due to the stay of Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar and his wife. This makes Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar, the Brahmin, very happy. Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar, the master of the Sagoda Yazh, and his wife rest there. Sagoda Yazh is one of the four types of Yazh. Periyazh, Makarayaazh, Sagodayaazh, and Sengottiyazh are the four types of Yazh. Among these, Sagoda Yazh has 14 strings. Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar always held this in his hand and was skilled in music. We will extensively see all the glories and characteristics of Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar in his Puranam and in the Puranam of Aludaiya Pillaiyar. Therefore, Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar and his wife rest there by divine grace, as divine grace is the cause of all these events. Thirugnanasambandha Peruman also rests there for the night. Thirugnanasambandha Peruman is a Jivanmukta (liberated soul) who transcends the waking state. Therefore, he is not subject to the states of Jagrat (waking), Svapna (dream), and Sushupti (deep sleep) like other beings. That is why Sekkizhar Peruman sings here, "He stayed in the house," and "the house remained." He does not explicitly state that Thirugnanasambandha Peruman rested but refers to it indirectly. That is, Sekkizhar Peruman first sings that Thirugnanasambandha Peruman stayed in Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's house. In this verse, he composes the verse describing the actions performed by Thirugnanasambandha Peruman in the early morning. Therefore, by omitting to describe Thirugnanasambandha Peruman's resting in between, Sekkizhar Peruman illustrates his Jivanmukta state to us. Thus, Thirugnanasambandha Peruman arrives. Then, he praises the Lord with the moon-adorned hair, who is enshrined in the Ayavanthi temple in Thiruchaththamangai, and sings a Thiruppathikam. In that Thiruppathikam, he glorifies Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar and sings that pure Tamil Thiruppathikam. This Thiruppathikam is for the holy place of Thiruchaththamangai. It is the 58th Thiruppathikam in the third Thirumurai of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman's Thevaram. In the second verse of this Thiruppathikam, Thirugnanasambandha Peruman has glorified Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar. Thirugnanasambandha Peruman sings, "The Lord who resides in Ayavanthi, whom Adigal Nakkan praises in Saaththamangai, a town of fragrant groves, with sacred ash on his chest and a sacred thread on one side, is united with the graceful woman." In this verse, Thirugnanasambandha Peruman glorifies Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar, stating that Lord Shiva resides in the Ayavanthi temple, where Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar worships Him in Thiruchaththamangai. This patikam is a Thiruppathikam sung by Thirugnanasambandha Peruman as he took leave from the Lord in Thiruchaththamangai. The Thiruppathikam sung by Thirugnanasambandha Peruman when he first arrived at Thiruchaththamangai is not available to us due to our lack of merit. Thus, Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, who praised the sacred feet of his Lord with a new garland of flowers in the form of this Thiruppathikam, bestowed his great friendship upon Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar. When it is said that Thirugnanasambandha Peruman sings a Thiruppathikam like a new garland of flowers, it means that he expresses the ancient Vedas in ever-new pure Tamil. Furthermore, only freshly bloomed flowers are specially suitable for puja. Therefore, since Thirugnanasambandha Peruman praises the Lord with different patikams in each holy place he visits, these patikams become a new garland of flowers. Thus, by singing, he bestows his exalted friendship upon Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar. This friendship is a bond of love and intimacy that resides in both their hearts as one. After this, it is also explained here the divine indication of the special privilege of Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar to perform the sacrificial rituals at Thirugnanasambandha Peruman's marriage and to accompany Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. Thus, Thirugnanasambandha Peruman now departs to visit other holy places. Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 11 — Tiruneelanakkar Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam by Sekkizhar Swamigal, Part 526, Thirunindra Sarukkam, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar Puranam. Thirugnanasambandha Peruman departs from Thiruchaththamangai to visit holy places and compose patikams. Sekkizhar Peruman composes the verse: "Having praised the Lord's feet with the four new flower-like patikams, and having bestowed abundant friendship upon Neelanakkar, the Brahmin who spoke the ancient Vedas in ever-new pure Tamil, he departed to the holy places that welcomed him." Thirugnanasambandha Peruman is praised here as "the Brahmin who spoke the ancient Vedas in ever-new pure Tamil." "Poosuran" means a celestial being living on this earth. "Poo" means earth, and "surar" means celestials. "Poosuran" means a celestial being living on this earthly world. This is a special epithet generally given to Brahmins. However, here, even though Thirugnanasambandha Peruman was born into a Brahmin lineage, the sacred name "Poosuran" is not merely a family name but also a name indicating his divine nature, his Shiva nature. All that he composed were ever-new pure Tamil ancient Vedic patikams. Here, Sekkizhar Peruman refers to Tamil as "ever-new pure Tamil." Sekkizhar Peruman's enthusiasm for the divine Tamil language is evident in many places, and this is one of the best examples. All the explanations we saw in the 970th verse, where he refers to divine Tamil as "Gnanam alandha menmai" (greatness that measured knowledge), are now worth recalling here. Tamil is eternal. Tamil is always new. Tamil bestows purity. These are all special characteristics of Tamil. Tamil is a language that has been in use throughout all ages. It is a language that is always new, without any deficiencies such as destruction, decay, increase, or decrease. Furthermore, it is a novel language because it always offers newness without the characteristic of being discarded as ancient. It is also interpreted as a divine language with unchanging youth. The Tamil language also has the glory of being "Kanni Tamil" (virgin Tamil). Thayumanavar Swamigal praises Goddess Parvathavarthini, enshrined in Rameswaram, saying, "O Mother who bore countless universes, yet the Vedas call you a virgin." This praise also applies to the Tamil language. Furthermore, Tamil is a language that bestows the imperishable bliss of liberation, which is called "semmai" (purity). The sacred sayings of the Samayachariyars and the philosophical texts of the Santhanachariyars were composed in Tamil, which is why the Tamil language possesses such glory. Generally, these characteristics belong to the Tamil language, but these epithets are especially applicable to the sacred sayings of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. Therefore, Thirugnanasambandha Peruman expresses the meanings of the ancient Vedas in new pure Tamil patikams. The Vedas are the sacred words spoken by Lord Shiva Himself. Here, the patikams composed by Pillaiyar are the divine words spoken by Lord Shiva through Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. Therefore, even though the Vedas and Thirugnanasambandha Peruman's Thevaram hymns are different in language, they are one in meaning and in the benefit they bestow. This truth is clearly evident to us here. Thirugnanasambandha Peruman mentions in the Thevaram of Ilambaiyangottur, "My word is His word." Therefore, the patikams composed by Thirugnanasambandha Peruman are the patikams spoken by Lord Shiva through Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. This is derived from this. Thus, as Thirugnanasambandha Peruman departed from Thiruchaththamangai to visit other holy places, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's love urged him to follow Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. Although that love and friendship were irresistible, Thirugnanasambandha Peruman commanded Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar to stay in Thiruchaththamangai. Therefore, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar, unable to disobey the divine command of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, found some peace of mind and remained in Thiruchaththamangai. Thus, the devotion and immense love that Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar had for Thirugnanasambandha Peruman's sacred words are clearly evident to us here. Thus, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar, the Vedic scholar who remained in Thiruchaththamangai, continued to perform the flawless worship of the Lord, praised by the Vedas, and the services to the devotees, even more excellently than before. Moreover, he continued to cultivate a deep sense of devotion to the beautiful sacred feet of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, the divine child of Lord Shiva. As many days passed in this manner, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar's love to be with Thirugnanasambandha Peruman grew intense. Therefore, Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar would visit the holy places where Thirugnanasambandha Peruman was present and have his darshan from time to time. Thus, he would spend some days with Thirugnanasambandha Peruman in the holy places where he was present, and then return to Thiruchaththamangai to continue his sacred service. Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar lived in this manner. Sekkizhar Peruman composes the 37th verse as follows: "The great ascetic Brahmin, who had performed great penance, with increasing love, after many days, attended the sacred marriage of the unique Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, who appeared in Sirkazhi, and along with him, attained the feet of the Lord." Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar, the great ascetic Brahmin, participated in and rejoiced at the sacred marriage of the incomparable Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, who appeared in Sirkazhi. He performed the sacrificial rituals at Thirugnanasambandha Peruman's marriage. Thus, by the good fortune of worshipping Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, he, along with him, attained the sacred feet of Lord Shiva. Now, the 38th verse is the concluding verse in the Puranam of Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar. The verse is: "Having worshipped the feet of Thiruneelanakkar, the great and foremost Brahmin who performed sacred duties in Saaththamangai, we shall now narrate the sacred service of Naminandhi Adigal, the twice-born Brahmin who, with a unified mind, understood the Lord who raised the bull banner." Sekkizhar Peruman, according to the rule he established, concludes the history of Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar, which he has narrated so far, in this concluding verse, and now introduces the next history in this verse. Having worshipped the sacred feet of Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar, the great and foremost Brahmin who appeared in Thiruchaththamangai and excelled in devotion, and with that support, Sekkizhar Peruman now begins to narrate the sacred service performed by Naminandhi Adigal, the twice-born Brahmin who lived with a unified mind, understanding Lord Shiva, who raised the bull banner. We too shall conclude the Puranam of Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar and, with the support of his sacred feet, begin to contemplate the Puranam of Naminandhi Adigal Nayanar. Praise to the sacred feet of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman! Praise to the sacred feet of Thiruneelanakkar Nayanar! Praise to the sacred feet of Thiruneelakanda Yazhpanar! Praise to the sacred feet of Sundaramurthi Swamigal! Praise to the sacred feet of Nambiyandar Nambi! Praise to the sacred feet of Sekkizhar Peruman! Praise to the sacred feet of Sivakavimani Ayya, who wrote the commentary on Periyapuranam! Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.
